  1344	
  1345	## 18. PAGE5: bounded products, fixed guards and exact vacancies
  1346	
  1347	Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-12 fix #12. PAGE5 extends the bounded PAGE
  1348	planner only where every admitted lane still has a constructive exhaustion
  1349	argument. It does not turn arbitrary Boolean authorization into a complete
  1350	query plan.
  1351	
  1352	**Bounded products.** The planner distributes independent positive
  1353	alternatives in the authorization rule across conjunctions before it
  1354	classifies the resulting lanes.
  1355	For example, two exact principal alternatives and three exact status
  1356	alternatives produce six lanes, each retaining both selected predicates and
  1357	the original query predicate. Distribution is all-or-nothing and remains
  1358	inside the existing eight-lane limit, including the system lane. Overflow,
  1359	negative alternatives and a residual second varying witness retain the
  1360	analyzer-limitation fallback.
  1361	
  1362	The same bounded Cartesian construction applies to positive membership PAGE
  1363	query filters. Every resulting branch must contain an eligible equality pin,
  1364	the system descriptor plus every party or membership descriptor must remain
  1365	within eight, and every original predicate stays in its lane. A product that
  1366	pins the membership witness's varying outer field, overflows, or leaves a
  1367	branch without a usable pin stays on the ordinary incomplete scan. The Lean
  1368	classifier constructs every factor before applying those same exclusions; it
  1369	never treats a failed product as an undistributed complete lane or a second
  1370	`or` predicate as absent. A non-alternative equality shared by every product
  1371	branch supplies an eligible pin to each branch.
  1372	
  1373	**Fixed grants beside one varying grant.** One dynamic membership witness may
  1374	be accompanied by one or more fixed `exists` guards, independent of authored
  1375	conjunction grouping. A fixed guard is admissible only when
  1376	the lane pins every field term in its target key and the target exposes a
  1377	required, total, unique point that is caller-readable after those exact lane
  1378	pins are substituted. The target must have no hidden source, read-through boundary,
  1379	`not before` boundary or expiry. The compiler substitutes the exact lane pin
  1380	into the guard and keeps that complete guard in the generated query filter;
  1381	an already-static point such as `exists roles[viewer]` needs no substitution
  1382	but remains in that filter. The residual may contain three or more fixed
  1383	guards; its exact short-circuit conjunction chain remains necessary in full.
  1384	The core validator independently matches it to the original rule expression
  1385	and lane pin. Thus the generated guard observes every fixed grant that could
  1386	authorize the authored rule. A hidden, timed, non-total or extra-equality
  1387	guard remains incomplete. Two varying grant sources also remain outside this
  1388	fragment.
  1389	
  1390	**Exact vacancy lanes.** The server may invert a top-level
  1391	`not exists holds[.id]` alternative when `holds.id` is a required, unique,
  1392	plain point and the paged table is online-only. Its vacancy lane scans the
  1393	same full ordered outer range as the system lane and privately authorizes each
  1394	candidate against the original rule; it publishes completion only after that
  1395	authorized walk is exhausted. A predicate-meter or authorization-walk cut
  1396	therefore produces an incomplete page. Filtered vacancy reads, synchronized
  1397	outer tables, compound or non-`id` keys, non-unique points and any shortcut
  1398	around the ordinary per-row authorization check keep the fallback. The target
  1399	may itself be hidden or timed: the private server walk evaluates the authored
  1400	rule's system probe for each candidate, then exposes only the authorized outer
  1401	row. This is a vacancy predicate over the outer row, not a claim that a private
  1402	target's absence is globally observable.
  1403	The intrinsic `id` point is always required and unique even though schema
  1404	column and unique lists omit it; other vacancy keys require both declarations.
  1405	
  1406	**Index rewrite note.** When every missing rule field is already fixed by an
  1407	independent exact query equality, the note prints one concrete declaration
  1408	using the actual table and collision-free index name, followed by the complete
  1409	rewritten query using that same index. The rewrite locates the focal read by
  1410	its source-map span, even when an earlier read uses the same index name, and
  1411	the binder recompiles the printed declaration and query together in tests.
  1412	When those exact equalities do not establish the rule prefix, the note
  1413	does not offer this rewrite.
  1414	
  1415	**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v4` fences the new validator
  1416	interpretations: fixed residual point guards and exact vacancy lanes. A device
  1417	that only understands `branch-pages-v3` must refuse this IR before decoding or
  1418	executing it; bounded products reuse the already validated positive-branch
  1419	lanes, and existing PAGE4 plans keep their prior capability. The Lean surface
  1420	classifier mirrors the three admissions and the private authorization walk.
  1421	Generated differential cases exercise the new lane families on native,
  1422	simulator and device hosts. Forms outside the fragment remain honest analyzer
  1423	limitations rather than completeness claims.

   123	                path.display()
   124	            ));

   126	        let mut child = Command::new(&path)

   128	            .stdout(Stdio::piped())
   129	            .stderr(Stdio::inherit())
   130	            .spawn()
   131	            .map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle {}: {e}", path.display()))?;
   132	        let stdin = child.stdin.take().unwrap();
   133	        let stdout = BufReader::new(child.stdout.take().unwrap());
   134	        Ok(Self { child, stdin, stdout })
   135	    }
   136	    pub fn ask(&mut self, case: &Json) -> Result<Json, String> {
   137	        writeln!(self.stdin, "{case}").map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle write: {e}"))?;
   138	        self.stdin.flush().map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle flush: {e}"))?;
   139	        let mut line = String::new();
   140	        let n = self.stdout.read_line(&mut line).map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle read: {e}"))?;
   141	        if n == 0 {
   142	            return Err("Lean oracle exited".into());
   143	        }
   144	        let answer: Json = serde_json::from_str(&line).map_err(|e| format!("Lean oracle answer {line:?}: {e}"))?;
   145	        if answer["kind"] == "error" {
   146	            // A case the model cannot decode is an emitter defect, never a verdict.
   147	            return Err(format!("Lean oracle refused the case: {}", answer["error"]));
   148	        }
   149	        Ok(answer)
   150	    }
   151	}
   152	impl Drop for LeanProcess {
   153	    fn drop(&mut self) {
   154	        let _ = self.child.kill();
   155	        let _ = self.child.wait();
   156	    }
   157	}
   158	
   159	thread_local! {
   160	    static PROCESS: RefCell<Option<LeanProcess>> = const { RefCell::new(None) };
   161	}
   162	
   163	/// Ask this thread's oracle process, starting it on first use and once
   164	/// more if it has died.
   165	pub fn ask(case: &Json) -> Result<Json, String> {
   166	    PROCESS.with(|cell| {
   167	        let mut slot = cell.borrow_mut();
   168	        if slot.is_none() {
   169	            *slot = Some(LeanProcess::spawn()?);
   170	        }
   171	        match slot.as_mut().unwrap().ask(case) {
   172	            Err(e) if e == "Lean oracle exited" => {
   173	                *slot = Some(LeanProcess::spawn()?);
   174	                slot.as_mut().unwrap().ask(case)
   175	            }
   176	            other => other,
   177	        }
   178	    })
   179	}
   180	
   181	/// The engine's value as the model's tagged value; rows as field maps.
   182	pub fn engine_value(v: &snapback4_core::Value) -> Json {
   183	    use snapback4_core::Value as V;
   184	    match v {
   185	        V::Null => json!({"null": null}),
   186	        V::Bool(b) => json!({"bool": b}),
   187	        V::Int(n) => json!({"int": n.to_string()}),
   188	        V::String(s) => json!({"text": s}),
   189	        V::Id(s) => json!({"id": s}),
   190	        V::Bytes(b) => json!({"bytes": b.iter().map(|x| format!("{x:02x}")).collect::<String>()}),
   191	        V::Row(fs) | V::Map(fs) => json!({"row": fs.iter().map(|(k, x)| (k.clone(), engine_value(x))).collect::<Map<_, _>>()}),
   192	        V::Array(xs) => json!({"arr": xs.iter().map(engine_value).collect::<Vec<_>>()}),
   193	    }
   194	}
   195	
   196	/// The model's tagged value with rows as field maps (its rows are ordered pairs).
   197	pub fn model_value(v: &Json) -> Json {
   198	    if let Some(xs) = v.get("arr").and_then(Json::as_array) {
   199	        return json!({"arr": xs.iter().map(model_value).collect::<Vec<_>>()});
   200	    }
   201	    if let Some(fs) = v.get("row").and_then(Json::as_array) {
   202	        let mut m = Map::new();
   203	        for p in fs {
   204	            m.insert(p[0].as_str().unwrap_or_default().to_owned(), model_value(&p[1]));
   205	        }
   206	        return json!({"row": m});
   207	    }
   208	    v.clone()
   209	}
   210	
   211	fn shape(v: &Json) -> String {
   212	    v.as_object().and_then(|o| o.keys().next().cloned()).unwrap_or_else(|| "value".into())
   213	}
   214	
   215	/// One side's comparable record: `kind`, then `code` or `data`/`complete`/`next`.
   216	#[derive(Clone, Debug, PartialEq)]
   217	pub struct Canon {
   218	    pub kind: String,
   219	    pub code: Option<String>,
   220	    pub data: Option<Json>,
   221	    pub complete: Option<bool>,
   222	    pub next: bool,
   223	}
   224	impl Canon {
   225	    /// The side's stable class for a differing `field`: the field's own
   226	    /// value where it is a flag, else a code, kind or data shape.
   227	    pub fn class(&self, field: &str) -> String {
   228	        if field.ends_with("complete") {
   229	            return if self.complete == Some(true) { "complete" } else { "incomplete" }.into();
   230	        }
   231	        if field.ends_with("next") {
   232	            return if self.next { "next" } else { "no-next" }.into();
   233	        }
   234	        match (&self.code, &self.data) {
   235	            (Some(code), _) => code.clone(),
   236	            (None, Some(d)) if self.kind == "answer" => shape(d),
   237	            _ => self.kind.clone(),
   238	        }
   239	    }
   240	    fn json(&self) -> Json {
   241	        json!({"kind": self.kind, "code": self.code, "data": self.data, "complete": self.complete, "next": self.next})
   242	    }
   243	}

     1	//! Merge sources share the statement loop's type checking and runtime domain.
     2	use super::*;
     3	use snapback4_core::ir::LaneSource;
     4	
     5	impl Binder<'_> {
     6	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#21.6 — query lanes take statement-for domains except walk; the meter bounds discovery.
     7	    pub(super) fn lane_domain(&mut self, domain: &A, out: &mut setup::Body) -> Fallible<(LaneSource, Ty)> {
     8	        if self.caller_kind != ProgramKind::Query && !matches!(domain, A::Read(_)) {
     9	            return Err(crate::diag::syntax_with(domain.site(), "range and list merge lanes belong in queries", "read the merge in a query; keep the job or mutation's existing page loop").guide("reads"));
    10	        }
    11	        let (bound, ty) = self.loop_domain(domain, out).map_err(|error| {
    12	            if matches!(error.code.as_str(), "E_UNBOUND_LOOP") || error.message.starts_with("for cannot iterate") || error.message.contains("static bound") {
    13	                crate::diag::syntax_with(domain.site(), "a merge for lane requires a prefix page, range(a, b), or a bounded list", "for h in range(hour(now) - 24, hour(now) + 1), for a in authors (authors: [principal] <=50), or for row in table[prefix] by index").guide("reads")
    14	            } else { error }
    15	        })?;
    16	        let source = match bound {
    17	            LoopDomain::Page(scan) => LaneSource::Scan(scan),
    18	            LoopDomain::LiteralRange { start, end_exclusive } => LaneSource::Range { start: ir::lit(Value::Int(start)), end_exclusive: ir::lit(Value::Int(end_exclusive)), max: Some(width(start, end_exclusive)) },
    19	            LoopDomain::ExpressionRange { start, end_exclusive } => LaneSource::Range { start, end_exclusive, max: range_width(domain) },
    20	            LoopDomain::ArgList { name, max } => LaneSource::List { value: ir::var(&name), max },
    21	            LoopDomain::DerivedArray { value, max, .. } => LaneSource::List { value, max },
    22	            LoopDomain::Recurse(_) => return Err(crate::diag::syntax_with(domain.site(), "a merge for lane requires a prefix page, range(a, b), or a bounded list; walk is not a lane domain", "for h in range(hour(now) - 24, hour(now) + 1), for a in authors (authors: [principal] <=50), or for row in table[prefix] by index").guide("reads")),
    23	        };
    24	        Ok((source, ty))
    25	    }
    26	}
    27	
    28	fn width(start: i64, end: i64) -> u64 { (i128::from(end) - i128::from(start)).max(0) as u64 }
    29	
    30	fn range_width(domain: &A) -> Option<u64> {
    31	    fn affine(value: &A) -> Option<(Option<&A>, i64)> {
    32	        if let Some(value) = literal_int(value) { return Some((None, value)); }
    33	        if let A::Binary { op, left, right, .. } = value {
    34	            if matches!(op.as_str(), "+" | "-") && let Some(n) = literal_int(right) {
    35	                let (base, offset) = affine(left)?;
    36	                return Some((base, if op == "+" { offset.checked_add(n)? } else { offset.checked_sub(n)? }));
    37	            }
    38	        }
    39	        // This comparison is for stable scalar expressions only, never reads,
    40	        // helper calls or new ids whose repeated evaluation may differ.
    41	        fn stable(value: &A) -> bool {
    42	            match value {
    43	                A::Name { .. } | A::Literal { .. } => true,
    44	                A::Unary { value, .. } => stable(value),
    45	                A::Binary { left, right, .. } => stable(left) && stable(right),
    46	                A::Call { name, args, .. } if matches!(name.as_str(), "day" | "hour" | "bucket" | "month" | "year" | "monthStart" | "yearStart") => args.iter().all(stable),
    47	                _ => false,
    48	            }
    49	        }
    50	        stable(value).then_some((Some(value), 0))
    51	    }
    52	    // @ref LLP 3000.007#14 — ruling 28's dynamic widths stay meter-bounded; only structurally equal stable bases admit offset folding.
    53	    // Source locations do not matter, but precedence does: the printer is not
    54	    // an identity for expressions such as (a + b) * 2 and a + b * 2.
    55	    fn same(a: &A, b: &A) -> bool {
    56	        match (a, b) {
    57	            (A::Name { name: a, .. }, A::Name { name: b, .. }) => a == b,
    58	            (A::Literal { value: a, .. }, A::Literal { value: b, .. }) => a == b,
    59	            (A::Unary { op: a, value: av, .. }, A::Unary { op: b, value: bv, .. }) => a == b && same(av, bv),
    60	            (A::Binary { op: a, left: al, right: ar, .. }, A::Binary { op: b, left: bl, right: br, .. }) => a == b && same(al, bl) && same(ar, br),
    61	            (A::Call { name: a, args: av, .. }, A::Call { name: b, args: bv, .. }) => a == b && av.len() == bv.len() && av.iter().zip(bv).all(|(a, b)| same(a, b)),
    62	            _ => false,
    63	        }
    64	    }
    65	    let A::Call { args, .. } = domain else { return None };
    66	    let (a, start) = affine(&args[0])?;
    67	    let (b, end) = affine(&args[1])?;
    68	    match (a, b) {
    69	        (None, None) => Some(width(start, end)),
    70	        (Some(a), Some(b)) if same(a, b) => Some(width(start, end)),
    71	        _ => None,
    72	    }
    73	}
    74	
    75	// A lane-dependent predicate needs the eager binding snapshot. Plain row
    76	// predicates can keep the same head strategy as explicitly written lanes.
    77	pub(super) fn filter_uses_lane(value: &A, name: &str) -> bool {
    78	    let read_uses_lane = |read: &ast::ReadExpr| {
    79	        read.keys.iter().any(|key| filter_uses_lane(&key.value, name))
    80	            || read.seeks.iter().any(|seek| filter_uses_lane(&seek.value, name))
    81	            || read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(|filter| filter_uses_lane(filter, name))
    82	            || read.matching.as_deref().is_some_and(|matching| filter_uses_lane(matching, name))
    83	            || read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_deref())
    84	                .is_some_and(|cursor| filter_uses_lane(cursor, name))
    85	            || read.computed.values().any(|value| filter_uses_lane(value, name))
    86	    };
    87	    match value {
    88	        A::Name { name: used, .. } => used == name,
    89	        A::Focal { .. } | A::Literal { .. } => false,
    90	        A::Member { base, .. } | A::Unary { value: base, .. } => filter_uses_lane(base, name),
    91	        A::Binary { left, right, .. } => filter_uses_lane(left, name) || filter_uses_lane(right, name),
    92	        A::Call { args, .. } => args.iter().any(|arg| filter_uses_lane(arg, name)),
    93	        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — an independent fixed point guard does
    94	        // not need a snapshot of the varying membership row.
    95	        A::Exists { read, .. } | A::Created { read, .. } | A::Read(read) => read_uses_lane(read),
    96	        _ => true,
    97	    }
    98	}

   195	/// Check `sources` with [`crate::compile`], then translate their surface
   196	/// trees. A program outside the fragment is recorded per program in

   199	pub fn lean(sources: &[(String, String)]) -> Result<LeanModule, LeanError> {
   200	    let bound = crate::compile(sources);
   201	    if !bound.diagnostics.is_empty() {
   202	        return Err(LeanError::Diagnostics(bound.diagnostics.iter().map(|d| format!("{}: {}", d.code, d.message)).collect()));
   203	    }

   205	    from_documents(&documents).map_err(LeanError::Unsupported)
   206	}

+            Expr::Binary { left, right, .. } => independent(left) && independent(right),
+            Expr::Unary { value, .. } => independent(value),
+            Expr::Array(values) => values.iter().all(independent),
+            Expr::Object(fields) => fields.values().all(independent),
+            Expr::Builtin { args, .. } => args.iter().all(independent),

+        .map(|first| first.to_uppercase().collect::<String>() + chars.as_str()).unwrap_or_default() };
+    let stem = leading.iter().filter(|column| column.as_str() != "id").map(|column| title(column)).collect::<String>();
+    if stem.is_empty() { return None; }
+    let mut name = format!("by{stem}");

diff --git a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/lanes.rs b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/lanes.rs
index 198e9455c7..c33f192dd7 100644
--- a/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/lanes.rs
+++ b/snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/program/lanes.rs
@@ -75,12 +75,24 @@ fn range_width(domain: &A) -> Option<u64> {
 // A lane-dependent predicate needs the eager binding snapshot. Plain row
 // predicates can keep the same head strategy as explicitly written lanes.
 pub(super) fn filter_uses_lane(value: &A, name: &str) -> bool {
+    let read_uses_lane = |read: &ast::ReadExpr| {
+        read.keys.iter().any(|key| filter_uses_lane(&key.value, name))
+            || read.seeks.iter().any(|seek| filter_uses_lane(&seek.value, name))
+            || read.filter.as_deref().is_some_and(|filter| filter_uses_lane(filter, name))
+            || read.matching.as_deref().is_some_and(|matching| filter_uses_lane(matching, name))
+            || read.range.as_ref().and_then(|range| range.cursor.as_deref())
+                .is_some_and(|cursor| filter_uses_lane(cursor, name))
+            || read.computed.values().any(|value| filter_uses_lane(value, name))
+    };
     match value {
         A::Name { name: used, .. } => used == name,
         A::Focal { .. } | A::Literal { .. } => false,
         A::Member { base, .. } | A::Unary { value: base, .. } => filter_uses_lane(base, name),
         A::Binary { left, right, .. } => filter_uses_lane(left, name) || filter_uses_lane(right, name),
         A::Call { args, .. } => args.iter().any(|arg| filter_uses_lane(arg, name)),
+        // @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — an independent fixed point guard does
+        // not need a snapshot of the varying membership row.
+        A::Exists { read, .. } | A::Created { read, .. } | A::Read(read) => read_uses_lane(read),
         _ => true,
     }
 }

+#[test]
+fn two_principal_status_scope_and_fixed_plus_per_row_grants_are_complete() {
+    let escorts = "table roles:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable escorts:\n  student: principal\n  volunteerOne: principal ?\n  volunteerTwo: principal ?\n  status: enum('requested', 'assigned', 'arrived', 'walking', 'completed')\n  at: int\n  read <- .student = viewer or exists roles[viewer] or ((.volunteerOne = viewer or .volunteerTwo = viewer) and (.status = 'assigned' or .status = 'arrived' or .status = 'walking'))\n  online only\nquery walks(c: cursor ?):\n  return escorts where (.volunteerOne = viewer or .volunteerTwo = viewer) and (.status = 'assigned' or .status = 'arrived' or .status = 'walking') first 1 by .at after c\n";
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), escorts.into())]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
+    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    e.seed([
+        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", Value::Null), ("status", Value::String("assigned".into())), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", id("alice")), ("status", Value::String("walking".into())), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
+        ("escorts".into(), object([("id", id("hidden")), ("student", id("s")), ("volunteerOne", id("alice")), ("volunteerTwo", Value::Null), ("status", Value::String("completed".into())), ("at", Value::Int(3))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "walks", "alice"), ["a", "b"]);
+
+    let grants = "table chapterAccess:\n  chapter: int\n  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versionAccess:\n  version: id\n  principal: principal\n  unique version, principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable versions:\n  chapter: int\n  at: int\n  read <- exists chapterAccess[.chapter, viewer] and exists versionAccess[.id, viewer]\n  online only\nquery versionsOf(chapter: int, c: cursor ?):\n  return versions where .chapter = chapter first 1 by .at after c\n";
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), grants.into())]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|n| matches!(n.code.as_str(), "N_NEVER_COMPLETE" | "N_CONDITIONAL_COMPLETE")), "{:?}", b.notes);
+    assert!(b.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
+    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    e.seed([
+        ("chapterAccess".into(), object([("id", id("ca")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
+        ("versionAccess".into(), object([("id", id("va")), ("version", id("a")), ("principal", id("alice"))])),
+        ("versions".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("versions".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("chapter", Value::Int(1)), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    let args = |chapter| BTreeMap::from([("chapter".into(), Value::Int(chapter)), ("c".into(), Value::Null)]);
+    let r = e.query("versionsOf", Context::new("alice", args(1))).unwrap();
+    assert!(r.complete, "{r:?}");
+    assert_eq!(r.data.as_array().unwrap().iter().map(|row| row.field("id").unwrap().as_str().unwrap()).collect::<Vec<_>>(), ["a"]);
+    let r = e.query("versionsOf", Context::new("bob", args(1))).unwrap();
+    assert!(r.complete && r.data.as_array().unwrap().is_empty(), "{r:?}");
+
+    // A rule probe sees its target with system authority, while the residual
+    // query probe uses ordinary read authority.  Keep PAGE out unless those
+    // observations are equivalent for a timeless readable exact point.
+    for unsafe_grant in [
+        grants.replacen("  read <- .principal = viewer", "  read <- deny", 1),
+        grants.replacen("  read <- .principal = viewer", "  until: time ?\n  expire at .until\n  read <- .principal = viewer", 1),
+        grants.replacen("  principal: principal\n  unique chapter, principal", "  principal: principal\n  active: bool\n  unique chapter, principal", 1)
+            .replace("exists chapterAccess[.chapter, viewer]", "exists chapterAccess[chapter = .chapter, principal = viewer, active = true]"),
+    ] {
+        let declined = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), unsafe_grant)]);
+        assert!(declined.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", declined.diagnostics);
+        assert!(declined.notes.iter().any(|n| n.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", declined.notes);
+    }
+}

+#[test]
+fn equality_filter_note_prints_one_matching_index_and_query() {
+    let source = "table skippers:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable watches:\n  crew: principal\n  starts: time\n  by byStart: starts, id\n  read <- .crew = viewer or exists skippers[viewer]\n  online only\nquery myWatches():\n  return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byStart\n";
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    let note = b.notes.iter().find(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE").unwrap();
+    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id"), "{note}");
+    assert!(note.rewrite.contains("return watches where .crew = viewer first 240 by byCrewStarts"), "{note}");
+
+    let repaired = source.replace("  by byStart: starts, id", "  by byStart: starts, id\n  by byCrewStarts: crew, starts, id")
+        .replace("first 240 by byStart", "first 240 by byCrewStarts");
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), repaired)]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
+}

+#[test]

+    let source = "table artists:\n  principal: principal\n  unique principal\n  read <- .principal = viewer\n  online only\ntable designs:\n  oneOfKind: bool\n  at: int\n  read <- exists artists[viewer] or not .oneOfKind or not exists holds[design = .id] or exists holds[design = .id, owner = viewer]\n  online only\ntable holds:\n  design: designs\n  owner: principal\n  expiresAt: time ?\n  unique design\n  read <- .owner = viewer or exists artists[viewer]\n  expire at .expiresAt\n  online only\nquery available(c: cursor ?):\n  return designs first 1 by .at after c\n";
+    let b = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), source.into())]);
+    assert!(b.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", b.diagnostics);
+    assert!(!b.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"), "{:?}", b.notes);
+    assert!(b.schema.required_capabilities().contains("branch-pages-v4"));
+    let mut e = Engine::memory(b.schema, b.programs.into_iter().map(|p| p.program)).unwrap();
+    e.seed([
+        ("artists".into(), object([("id", id("artist")), ("principal", id("art"))])),
+        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("a")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(false)), ("at", Value::Int(1))])),
+        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("b")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(true)), ("at", Value::Int(2))])),
+        ("designs".into(), object([("id", id("c")), ("oneOfKind", Value::Bool(true)), ("at", Value::Int(3))])),
+        ("holds".into(), object([("id", id("h")), ("design", id("c")), ("owner", id("alice")), ("expiresAt", Value::Null)])),
+    ]).unwrap();
+    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "bob"), ["a", "b"]);
+    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "alice"), ["a", "b", "c"]);
+    assert_eq!(walk(&mut e, "available", "art"), ["a", "b", "c"]);
+
+    let non_point = source.replace("  design: designs\n", "  design: designs ?\n")
+        .replace(" or exists holds[design = .id, owner = viewer]", "");
+    let declined = compile(&[("schema.q".into(), non_point)]);
+    assert!(declined.diagnostics.is_empty(), "{:?}", declined.diagnostics);
+    assert!(declined.notes.iter().any(|note| note.code == "N_NEVER_COMPLETE"));
+}

+/// A generated membership lane may retain prefix-fixed point grants
 /// as a residual predicate.  Recognize only the exact bound point expression:
 /// this proves that every row emitted by the lane satisfies that conjunct,
 /// while a missing, reordered or merely similar probe keeps the merge out.
-// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — one fixed grant may guard one varying per-row grant.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — fixed grants may guard one varying per-row grant.
 fn fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema: &Schema, filter: &Expr, rule: &RuleExpr,
     pins: &[(&String, &Expr)]) -> bool {
     if let Expr::Binary { op: crate::ir::BinaryOp::And, left, right } = filter {
         return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
             || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
     }
+    // The IR preserves short-circuit `a and b` as a tiny Boolean block.
+    // Accept only that exact lowering: both operands are necessary, while an
+    // `or` block assigns on the false branch and cannot establish either one.
+    if let Expr::Block { body, value } = filter
+        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Let { name, value: left }, crate::ir::Stmt::If {
+            condition: Expr::Variable(condition), then_body, else_body }] = body.as_slice()
+        && let [crate::ir::Stmt::Assign { name: assigned, value: right }] = then_body.as_slice()
+        && else_body.is_empty() && condition == name && assigned == name
+        && matches!(value.as_ref(), Expr::Variable(result) if result == name) {
+        return fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, left, rule, pins)
+            || fixed_filter_requires_rule(schema, right, rule, pins);
+    }
     let (Expr::Exists(read), RuleExpr::Exists { table, index, key, equalities, .. }) = (filter, rule) else { return false };
     let Some(target) = schema.table(table) else { return false };
     let Some(point) = target.index(index) else { return false };
     equalities.is_empty() && target.through.is_none() && target.not_before.is_none() && !target.is_expiring()
         && point.unique && point.is_plain() && point.components.iter().all(|part| part.column()
             .and_then(|column| target.columns.get(column)).is_some_and(|kind| !kind.is_optional()))
-        && schema.rule_input_is_readable(table, index, key)
         && &read.table == table && &read.index == index && read.key.len() == key.len()
         && read.key.iter().zip(key).all(|(expression, term)| rule_term_expression(term, expression, pins))
+        && point_read_is_visible(schema, read)
+}
+
+/// A rule probe uses system authority, but the retained residual point uses
+/// ordinary caller authority. Re-prove that the generated, pin-substituted
+/// point is visible instead of asking about the unsubstituted authored terms.
+fn point_read_is_visible(schema: &Schema, read: &crate::ir::Read) -> bool {
+    let Some(table) = schema.table(&read.table) else { return false };
+    let Some(index) = table.index(&read.index) else { return false };
+    if !index.is_plain() || index.components.len() != read.key.len() { return false; }
+    let pinned = |name: &str| index.components.iter().zip(&read.key).find_map(|(part, value)|
+        (part.column().is_some_and(|column| column == name)).then_some(value));
+    visibility(&read.table, table, &table.rules.read, &pinned).decided_true
 }

+/// A membership PAGE with two independent `or` filters stays on the ordinary
+/// incomplete scan. The native membership planner distributes at most one
+/// such filter; the independent model must not silently discard the second.
+// @ref LLP 3000.036.001#18 — undistributed query alternatives are outside the constructive PAGE fragment.
+pub fn page_membership_filter_fallback(seed: u64) -> Case {
+    let mut case = base(seed);
+    case.program.tables[0].rule = Rule::OwnerOrWitness;
+    case.program.tables[0].effects = None;
+    case.program.tables[0].residency = Residency::Online;
+    let access = case.program.tables.iter_mut().find(|table| table.name == "access").unwrap();
+    access.rule = Rule::Allow; access.effects = None; access.residency = Residency::Public;
+    access.expire = None; access.not_before = None;
+    case.program.body = Body::Read { kind: Read::Page, take: 1, descending: seed % 2 == 0,
+        filter: false, owner: false, project: None };
+    case.program.domain = Some("(.bucket = bucket or .bucket = 1)".into());
+    case.program.residual = Some("(.score > 0 or .flag = false)".into());
+    case.program.order = Some(vec!["label".into(), "score".into()]);
+    case.program.named_page_order = None; case.program.prelude.clear(); case.program.lets.clear();
+    case.steps.clear(); case.rows.clear();
+    case
+}

+#[test]
+fn membership_page_with_two_filter_alternatives_stays_incomplete_in_lean_and_native() {

  1071	## 16. PAGE3: finish the ordinary private-page plans

  1076	PAGE2 left 18 notes in 11 of the 50 odd wave-10 final schemas: 12
  1077	party/static-grant pages, three compound rules, two hidden parent sources
  1078	and one pure membership. This amendment extends the existing plan rather

  1080	replicated data or runtime resource ceiling.
  1081	
  1082	**Static grants and whole-group reads.** An owner-or-readable-static-grant
  1083	rule uses the existing party/grant merge on synced union tables too. The
  1084	server evaluates the positive readable grant once; its holder reads the
  1085	original whole range, everyone else the party ranges. An explicit `first` or
  1086	`last` is a page bound, not a table cap: a role holder may page an unbounded
  1087	table, under the usual request meter. An implicit whole-group read is expanded
  1088	only if its original fixed prefix has an applicable stored cap or fully
  1089	pinned total unique, at or below the examined ceiling. That original bound
  1090	applies to the union. A cap on owner does not bound a staff's whole table.
  1091	A fully pinned total unique may supply a one-row lane with the implicit id
  1092	position, even when its physical index has no unpinned component.
  1093	
  1094	**Full union devices.** The same descriptor and scope serve server and device
  1095	cursors. On a device a full union target's grant lane reads its held authorized
  1096	rows, with the ordinary acquisition, coverage and meter checks; it does not
  1097	probe a remote grant. That held range covers every other target lane, so the
  1098	others are not opened. Missing acquisition stays unknown. Online-only and
  1099	single-arm narrowed sync retain their existing grant deferral. This amends
  1100	LLP 3000.036.000.000 §5.3's planner restriction, while preserving its holding
  1101	and acquisition invariants.
  1102	
  1103	**Pure membership.** Remove the syntactic party requirement from §15. A rule
  1104	`exists members[.id, viewer]` or `exists members[.parent, viewer]` has a system
  1105	range and exact membership descriptors, no party descriptors. All source
  1106	readability, unique/cap proofs, total filters, index/key bounds, shared
  1107	positions and discovery/head fences remain. If the source discovery is cut,
  1108	no target rows or cursor are emitted. An empty complete discovery means an
  1109	empty complete page. Point uniqueness still does not cap memberships.
  1110	
  1111	**Positive compound rules.** Distribute positive `and`/`or` into at most seven
  1112	alternatives plus the original system range. Each alternative retains all
  1113	of its row equality conditions. It contains either one exact membership
  1114	witness, or row equality pins and positive row-independent guards. A membership
  1115	conjunct supplies precisely its own authority; all remaining conjuncts must
  1116	be true by the target lane's pins. A pinned membership target instead uses
  1117	a readable page guard, without discovering hidden source rows. No negated
  1118	probe, arithmetic, fallible predicate or second varying source component is
  1119	admitted. Candidates are speculative and fall back when the physical plan
  1120	or bound-IR validator cannot prove the whole admitted shape.
  1121	
  1122	A new guarded-range descriptor evaluates `page_guard_decision` on its own
  1123	pins. A true readable guard opens only that range, not the other alternatives;
  1124	a false guard opens none. It does not cover other lanes. The system range
  1125	covers every lane only for the system. Status/party lanes, discovered parent
  1126	lanes and guarded lanes share one ordered id-tied position and deduplicate
  1127	there, as in §§3–6. Thus if `R = ∨i (Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi)` and W is the total page
  1128	predicate, each lane returns exactly `W ∧ Pi ∧ Gi ∧ Mi`; their union is W ∧ R.
  1129	Guard facts and membership sources must be readable, as in §15, so neither
  1130	empty results nor completeness reveal hidden witness rows. Read-meter cuts
  1131	and frontier rules are unchanged; heap exhaustion remains a refusal.
  1132	
  1133	A nullable small enum's `field != null` has a finite positive expansion into
  1134	its declared non-null values. This retains null exclusion exactly; ordinary
  1135	nullable inequalities against other values keep their original scan. The
  1136	wave-10 archive with four species therefore gets four enum ranges plus its
  1137	owner, readable reviewer and system ranges. Compound volunteer-open and

  1139	
  1140	**Readable static predicates.** A `where` disjunction may repeat a readable
  1141	static point, such as `.gleaner = viewer or exists coordinators[viewer]`.
  1142	Every point-key expression must be an infallible, read-free name or literal
  1143	(including canonical signed numeric literals); arithmetic, assertions, calls

  1146	its identical self-witness rule, without a lifetime or read-through boundary.
  1147	It remains in the original predicate for every caller, including the system.
  1148	This extension belongs to static party/where branch merges only. Membership
  1149	discovery and compound-rule expansion retain their read-free total filter
  1150	requirement, mirrored separately by model cases 3 and 4. The physical point
  1151	can cut even though its logical predicate is total. The

  1155	
  1156	A discovered/guard-admitted merge range pays its admission reservation once
  1157	on both runtime hosts, including empty ranges. Device acquired-row trust does
  1158	not add a second prefix-rule reservation to an already admitted lane.
  1159	
  1160	**Compatibility and model.** `branch-pages-v2` fences guarded descriptors,
  1161	held-union merge behavior and fully pinned point-lane admission; it is derived

  1215	
  1216	Commissioned 2026-10-14 for wave-11 fix #11. This amendment extends the
  1217	existing bounded planner. No read authority, replicated audience, runtime
  1218	ceiling or cursor codec changes. Query unions containing a public status
  1219	are separate from union *sync*: a public sync branch remains excluded.
  1220	
  1221	**Mixed positive alternatives.** Positive distribution also applies to a
  1222	simple disjunction mixing party equality, public/status equality, exact
  1223	membership and readable static grants. Each alternative retains every
  1224	conjunct and the complete original query predicate. Equality lanes,
  1225	guarded ranges and exact discovered membership lanes share the existing
  1226	ordered id-tied merge, with duplicate positions emitted once. At most seven
  1227	alternatives plus the system range fit. Speculative source/target indexes
  1228	are retained only when the bound validator accepts the whole plan.
  1229	
  1230	Named plain page indexes normalize to their declared column order only in
  1231	a speculative merge candidate. A declined candidate keeps its original read.
  1232	An exact owner/member rule whose varying component is necessarily pinned
  1233	can use the existing party/guard merge; alternative-only pins do not qualify.
  1234	
  1235	**Exact where witnesses.** A positive whole `where` conjunct identical to a
  1236	whole read-rule disjunct can define an exact narrower audience: for example
  1237	`posts where exists authors[.id, viewer] first 20 by .at after c`. Discover
  1238	that readable witness under §§14–16's total point and complete-discovery
  1239	proofs. Other audiences cannot contribute rows to W. Every other conjunct
  1240	must be read-free and total. The original predicate remains in admission
  1241	validation; the system descriptor retains the authored witness, because
  1242	system bypasses R but never W. Its prefix also binds the fixed target pins
  1243	into the shared cursor scope. A hidden source keeps the original scan.
  1244	
  1245	**Pinned components of exact witnesses.** A positive alternative may pin
  1246	one component of a multi-column rule probe. Substitute only a necessary
  1247	infallible fixed equality, leaving one varying source component; the core
  1248	validator independently checks the original field term against that exact
  1249	target prefix. Example: `.manager = viewer and exists reporting[report =
  1250	.report, manager = .manager]` discovers reporting rows with manager=viewer,
  1251	then their report values. The other party's alternative inverts the other
  1252	component. Extra named point equalities survive. No arithmetic, missing
  1253	pin, second varying component or hidden source is treated as proof.

  1256	point-read row image is a fixed lexical value, not a new row read. In a

  1260	not prove that a child's copied band/manager equals its parent's field;

VERDICT: NOT READY

1. **Supported Boolean pins are rejected by the independent emitter gate.** At `fa6c1fdf68:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs:42`, `page_pin` accepts only explicit `=` expressions. Native `intent::equality` and Lean `Expr.eqPin` also recognize bare Boolean conjuncts. For an exact membership rule, `where .flag and (.score > 0 or .score > 10)` produces two valid membership branches sharing `.flag = true`, but the emitter reports `page-membership-filter-product` and executes zero comparisons. Recognize the same Boolean pins and add a positive comparison regression.

2. **The new descriptor limit overcounts repeated party alternatives.** At `fa6c1fdf68:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs:100`, parties are collected without deduplication, then counted at line 121. A rule `.owner = viewer or .owner = viewer or exists access[.bucket, viewer]` with three eligible filter alternatives has seven native descriptors; the gate counts ten and incorrectly refuses comparison. Deduplicate party columns before counting, matching native planning and Lean’s `parties.eraseDups`.

3. **The printed equality repair can fail recompilation on portable key bounds.** At `fa6c1fdf68:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/bind/mod.rs:985`, the helper checks component count but neither encoded key size nor label size before emitting the declaration at line 1013. In the existing watches example, changing `starts` to `text <=430` leaves `byStart: starts, id` valid at 1,999 bytes, but the suggested `crew, starts, id` requires 2,137 bytes and fails with `E_INDEX_KEY_BYTES`. Apply the compiler’s portable key and label checks before offering the repair; cover this boundary with a printed-pair recompilation regression.

2. **The new descriptor limit overcounts repeated party alternatives.** At `fa6c1fdf68:snapback4/crates/snapback4-lang/src/emit/lean/pages.rs:100`, parties are collected without deduplication, then counted at line 121.
